## Per-decision (Y)

### Y1 — What this CURRENT rewrite authorizes

**Question:** The uncommitted Objective is a concierge web-app / project recovery service. Git HEAD is dest millwork. Yesterday’s proposal is APK `you-decide-ritual` with website later. PRODUCT.md says anphuni.com Session is not core. Which is *this* CURRENT rewrite?

**Options presented:**

1. Concierge / project-recovery service is this halt. Next after apply = `publish-pilot-page`. Park APK millwork as following. Recant “anphuni.com later” for this Domain.
2. APK ritual is still this halt. Recant the concierge Objective. Restore `you-decide-ritual`. Website following.
3. Sequence, one id. This conversation only finishes the CURRENT draft. After stamp, Next = `publish-pilot-page`. APK parked, not deleted. Not both in one Action list.
4. Dual door. Pilot page and APK millwork as one Next. Forbidden.

**Selected:** **1** (2026-09-09 Round 1). Operator override of rec 3. Gloss: dual door (4) “already is reject”.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Was **3** (finish draft this sitting, then stamp). Operator picked **1**: concierge *is* this halt, not a drafting-only sequence. APK millwork following. Dual-Next stays Reject.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** (1) hours of law + a page, $0 extra SaaS if anphuni.com already exists. (2) millwork hours already in-tree; website wait. (3) cheaper *process* (grill only) but delays the door. (4) two engineer-weeks and a broken gate.
- **alpha (50):** (1) need an intake that is not “accounts” if that stays Prohibited. (2) 50 USB sideloads. (4) two faces.
- **growth (1,000):** hosted 500MB×N is a storage product; protocol folders stay $0 self-host.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** concierge on anphuni.com is hosted. Core protocol stays self-host. Dollar delta at v1: ~$0 if the domain already runs; at 1,000 users storage dominates unless files stay in the client’s folder.
- **Engineer-time:** (1) as execute = days after stamp. (3) this sitting = grill + propose (hours). (2) continues the APK sprint. (4) forbidden.
- **Crossover:** recant to (2) only if the Objective is withdrawn. Recant to (3) only if this sitting must not name `publish-pilot-page` as live Next until a later stamp.

**Dependencies:** none (root). Unlocks Y4 (auto), Y5, Y8, Y9 (auto), Y10, Y11. Parks APK millwork.

**Status:** `decided`

### Y2 — Draft-in-slot vs already stamped

**Question:** Header Approval is APPROVED while Baseline is `needs /grill`, Keep is incomplete, Next allowed action is `...`, and the Approval condition still says copy onto live Domain. `preflight publish-pilot-page` would ALLOW. Is this file stamped law, or a draft sitting in the CURRENT slot?

**Options presented:**

1. Draft in the slot. SELECT / DRAFT / PENDING until you stamp a complete file. **Recommended.**
2. Already live law. Keep APPROVED. Completing Keep is a patch. Agents may execute header Next.
3. Wrapper only. Move text to `.aether/proposals/` and restore HEAD CURRENT until stamp.

**Selected:** **1** (2026-09-09 Round 1) with operator gloss **already REJECTED**. Rec was PENDING; disk is SELECT / REJECTED / REJECTED after human `aether reject` (“Just don't feel like it.”). Already-stamped APPROVED is Reject.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Was **1** (PENDING). Operator picked 1 and had already rejected the unfinished stamp. Completing the draft is a new SELECT after reject, then a later Yes. Not option 2.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** (1) $0; honest gate. REJECTED is stronger than PENDING for “do not treat as Yes.” Probe still ALLOW on Next match — that is a protocol quirk, not a Yes. (2) accidental execute. (3) git restore hours.
- **alpha / growth:** a false stamp at 50–1,000 is a service running without a Keep.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** n/a.
- **Engineer-time:** already spent (human reject). Completing Keep still required before a real approve.
- **Crossover:** move to PENDING on the *proposal* when the draft is complete; live disk stays REJECTED until the human stamps.

**Dependencies:** independent of Y1. Couples to when `aether approve` is honest.

**Status:** `decided`

### Y3 — SPEC-valid header vs custom labels

**Question:** Phase `SERVICE ENTRY` and Status `ACTIVE ― NOT YET VALIDATED` are not SPEC-v0.2 enums. Validate is OK because the parser is lenient. Do we keep custom labels, map to SPEC, or extend aether this halt?

**Options presented:**

1. SPEC-valid header. SERVICE ENTRY lives in Objective/Offer. Phase/Status/Approval stay in the enum. **Recommended.**
2. Keep custom labels. Lifecycle mapping is informal.
3. Extend schema this halt. Teach aether new tokens.

**Selected:** **1** (2026-09-09 Round 1). Disk Phase already SELECT. SERVICE ENTRY stays a human label in Objective/Offer, not **Phase:**.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Was **1**. Operator matched.

## Per-decision (C)

### C1 — Product this rewrite authorizes

**Question:** The live APPROVED Objective is a stranger Play APK (**Mechanicall: One Next**, quiet home, no bind, app-private store). You just said you want to *push it from that* to **the phone as control surface for a bound project folder**. Those are two products. Which one is *this* CURRENT rewrite?

**Options:**
1. **Bound-folder sit is this Next.** Recant store-first. Phone binds a project folder (not mechanicall-os). Plan/Draft/Decide/Receipt on that folder. One Next Play is following, not live.
2. **Keep One Next store-first.** Sit is archived lab (“You Decide”). Bound folder is *not* the store face. (Matches today’s APPROVED header; contradicts what you just said.)
3. **Sequence, one id at a time.** This Next = bound-folder sit look (halt). Following id = One Next Play scaffold. Not both in one Action list.
4. **One Next *is* the bound folder** (SAF/all-files on the store APK). Contradicts live Reject (`MANAGE_EXTERNAL_STORAGE` on One Next store build) and Maya “drop all-files”.

**Selected:** **1** (2026-09-05 Round 2 answer). Bound-folder sit is this Next. Recant store-first. One Next Play following.  
**Recommendation:** was **1** or **3**; operator picked **1**.  
**Cost tradeoff:** (1)/(3) $0 sideload, millwork already exists, testers need a folder not a store page. (2) 2–6 weeks new shell + Play Console; sit polish stops. (4) Play review risk (all-files) + $25 + likely reject. Dual of 1+2 in one Next = two engineer-weeks and a broken gate.  
**Dependencies:** none (root). Unlocks C4 millwork, C7 (do not archive sit as dead), C9 bind how. Conflicts C2=3 until C2b.  
**Status:** decided

# M-series (parked under C) — what is the actual MVP

**Skill (historical):** `/grill-me-store-decisions` (one question at a time + persist) · in-repo grilling (frontier; facts from disk).  
**Premise (2026-08-25):** Operator: “I feel like I've actually overprogrammed the app — grill me on the actual MVP.”  
**2026-08-25 dump (not yet law):** Plan = typeset CURRENT (no md glyphs / nested scroll / Changes); Draft = interactive Plan + schemas + TBC; models deferred; login/preauth baked in APK not Compose window; SOS plugin as production chrome; one network = folder upload/download; JOIN/WAKE overlaid “under construction”; produce UI the same way the original plugin UI was produced.

**Interview started:** 2026-08-25  
**Interview status:** **OPEN (M4/M5 parked).** M-series product locked. **M3=2** (`sit-mvp-rack`). Human already re-SELECT’d header Next. Body still distro-gate. Propose: `.aether/proposals/CURRENT-proposal-20260825-1821.md`.  
**Does not recant** `sit-conjure-desk` until human applies a CURRENT.  
**Halt:** human reviews Imagine PNGs + CRect map before assemble. This grill does **not** Imagine a sit until CURRENT allows leftover-chrome.

## Guiding principles (M)

1. CURRENT is law. This grill does not `aether next` / rewrite CURRENT.
2. One Next. Dual-Next Reject.
3. Models never approve. Silence ≠ permission. Decide is the only Yes.
4. **Overprogrammed** (this sitting): treat extra surface as guilty until it earns a place in MVP.
5. Minimize paid third parties. No BaaS as authority. Play $25 is already the tester path if testers are MVP.
6. Phone bind ≠ this operator tree.
7. PRODUCT: core is the filesystem protocol; the phone sit is the **lab face**.
8. **M1=6:** model *deployment* deferred this MVP (auto-draft-from-prompt is a TBC well, not a ship).
9. **M1=6:** chrome produced like a 2005 VST editor (baked panel bitmap in the binary + overlay hits), not live Compose millwork.
10. **M8=3:** the networking that earns MVP is Headscale/tsnet **carrying** the folder zip. JOIN/WAKE/SEND are **symbolic static PNG plates** (Imagine, freeze) — recant verbose Compose status/preauth well, not recant the mesh. Funnel still Reject. tsnet JNI gap must close for this to be true.
11. **M2:** entire sit UI is **generated plates**; live data *fills* plates (do not invent a new information architecture). Plan and Draft are simplified work areas (published vs workshop), not chip museums.
12. **Human reviews plate PNGs + CRect map** before any Go / assemble. Silence ≠ permission.
13. **M7=1:** Imagine is **Photoshop**, not the spec. FACE-SPEC “no Imagine-mocks as the spec” **stands**. Fun is paint; ship is freeze into `drawable-nodpi/`. Not Camel clones.
14. **M10=1:** AuthKey is compiled into the APK so JOIN has no Compose window. Secret **never in git**. Assemble injects from `~/.mechanicall/headscale-preauth` (or sibling of the Play keystore). Treat the APK as public: ephemeral + `tag:stranger` + TTL + ACL. No R8-as-security. ControlURL may be baked (not a secret).
15. **M11=2:** WAKE is a symbolic PNG whose hit-rect still POSTs wol-pi. Conjure (S3, HOLD 15, doorbell) is **in this MVP** with Imagine plates + tsnet. Recants M1=6’s “conjure is not the done-line.” Dual-Next still Reject — one fat Next.

### M0b — Camel Space SOS + how the original plugin UI was produced (auto)

**Selected (auto):** Production-grade plugin UI is a **baked panel**, not live layout.

**What Camel Space actually is.** Camel Audio *CamelSpace* (Musikmesse 2005; SOS magazine “SOS Test” preset photographed Nov 2005). Rhythmic multi-effect: 128-step trance gate (vol/pan/cutoff), MM filter, auto-pan, flanger, stereo delay, reverb, enhancer, XY, Randomize. Apple later folded the *sound* into Logic Step FX; the *editor* is the thing we steal. The in-tree photo is `dev/39_sit-storm-factory/13_camel-sos-check/references/camel-space-sos.jpg` (DAW title `Guitar 2: Ins. 1 - CamelSpace`, preset **SOS Test**). Grammar: overlapping brushed-metal plates, LCD well, GATE pits, filmstrip knobs, host window is the DAW — the plugin fills that window.

**How that UI was produced (2004–2006 VST shop, not Compose):**

| Stage | Tool | What lands in the *binary* |
|-------|------|----------------------------|
| Paint | Photoshop: noise → motion blur → lighting. Later SkinMan / KnobMan (2007+) for filmstrips; POV-Ray for photographed knobs | Whole-panel PNG/BMP + per-widget frames |
| Toolkit | **VSTGUI 2.x** `CBitmap` / `CAnimKnob` / `CMovieBitmap` | One background bitmap; knobs = vertical filmstrips; hits = `CRect`s, not Layout |
| Pack (Win) | PE `.rsrc` (`RT_BITMAP`, custom `PNG`/`PICTURE`/`RCDATA`) | Resource Hacker / Restorator |
| Pack (Mac) | `.vst` `Contents/Resources/` | Loose PNG or Mach-O bytes |
| Later (not Camel Space) | JUCE `BinaryData.cpp` | Alchemy-era. Not 2005. |

The host (DAW / Android OEM) only owns the window. The plugin **blits the editor**. Lighting, AO, overlapping plates, rivets, LCD glass were decided in Photoshop and **frozen**. Live rectangles with a repeating metal shader are the pastiche.

**Android analogue already named, built, then reverted:**

```
scripts/render-sit-skin.py          # inspectable PSD
  → res/drawable-nodpi/sit_skin.png # CBitmap (no density scale)
  → Compose Image fill              # blit
  → transparent hit-targets         # CRects
```

`15_binary-skin` shipped that (`BINARY-PIPELINE.md`). `16_revert-skin` undid it because you said the cream/LCD/GATE face was “fine now” and the reskin would “probably fail” (**P2b**). Live APK has **no** `drawable-nodpi/sit_skin.png`. Live millwork is Compose again. CURRENT still **Rejects** `SOS-reskin-as-this-Next` and `leftover-compose-millwork-as-sos`. FACE-SPEC v2: rack around paper; Plan = published CURRENT; Draft = Suggesting; JOIN paste is a **Compose** `OutlinedTextField` (“preauth / login-server · not stored”); `JOIN ${status}` / `WAKE ${status}` are live labels (`not-on-net`, `sleeping`).

**Copyright:** steal the *pipeline*, never Camel Audio bitmaps (FACE-SPEC V7 FAIL; CURRENT Prohibited `camel-png-theft`).

**Cost tradeoff:** $0 to name. Re-asking “how did Camel Audio make the GUI?” wastes the grill. Re-landing blit is 1–2 engineer-weeks and is the same job that was recanted.

**Dependencies:** M0. Unlocks M1 option 6, M-face (pipeline vs Compose), M-login (baked vs well).

**Status:** `decided` (auto)

Live DRAFT is field chips + LIVE/SUGGESTING + composer (`ChatHome.kt`); first-sit still says “One prompt makes a template.” `AUTHORITY_FIELDS` on disk are only Objective / Phase / Status / Baseline / Next / Approval — not Keep / Reject / Limits. Camel Space **does not scroll**; the editor is one fixed bitmap. Live operator CURRENT.md is ~100 lines. You cannot blit an arbitrary markdown file the way SOS blits a filter. How does Plan/Draft actually lay out?

**Options presented:**

1. **Fixed plates (SOS).** PLAN = CRect paper of *typeset fields* (at least Objective, Next, Keep, Reject, Limits — labels, not `**Next:**`). No Changes, no Proposal, no nested millwork window. Overflow = MORE / section plates (like plugin modules), not a Compose scroll-window. DRAFT = the same plates, editable, schema visible, TBC well **empty** this MVP (no 7B). Recants B2. **Recommended.**
2. **Typeset whole file; paper still scrolls.** Kill Changes + Proposal + md glyphs. One document scroll as paper (honest length). Recants B2’s Changes; keeps document overflow. Weaker SOS.
3. **Keep B2.** PLAN still has Changes + Proposal; just prettier typesetting. Contradicts the dump and M1=6.
4. **One bank, published/edit toggle.** No separate PLAN/DRAFT tabs. Published = typeset; edit = schema + TBC. Collapses B4’s two-module walk.
5. **LCD-only Plan.** PLAN shows Next + Objective on the rack LCD; full CURRENT only in Draft. Too little “published CURRENT.md.”

**Selected:** **1 — fixed plates**, with user override (2026-08-25): *entire UI* is generated plates; smart-fill from current sit data (do not invent new IA); Plan and Draft stay as specified (simplified work areas: published typeset vs interactive schema + TBC); **implementation framework needs human review before Go**. Not 2–5.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Option **1**. You picked SOS *and* “no scroll window.” Those two only cohere if PLAN is a **view of fields**, not a printout of the file. The override extends plates to the whole rack (title / LCD / GATE / DESK / tabs / paper / bind / splash), which is what `SKIN-LAYOUT.md` already named. Option 2 is the honest document and the weaker plugin. Option 3 is a recant of the dump. Option 4 saves a tab and loses the published/workshop split. Option 5 starves PLAN. First-sit 7B is **off** under M1=6; TBC is a well, not a generate. `app_verify.py` / BEHAVIOURS B2–B4 rewrite in the implementing Next — after framework review, not in this grill.

**Cost tradeoff:**

### M9 — How does preauth enter if JOIN is a static PNG? — **decided (intent)**

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Original dump: login/pre-auth “baked into the APK, not some Compose window.” M8=3: Headscale carries the folder; JOIN/SEND are symbolic frozen PNGs (recant verbose well). Fact: tsnet `AuthKey` is pasted preauth, RAM only; `ControlURL` is the Headscale login-server; state is `filesDir/tsnet/`, never the bound folder (`android/tsnet/README.md`). Until the AAR exists, JOIN cannot be `connected`. A static PNG cannot type a key. Compiling the key into the APK is extractable and fights Limits (no secrets in git; APK is a zip). 3. **Provision sidecar / QR (gitignored).** Operator mints; sitter never types. JOIN plate is only a lamp. Closest to “baked” without putting the secret in git.
4. **Share-intent the key onto the JOIN plate.** No text field; Android paste/share. Same as 1 with a different hit.
5. **Keep the Compose window.** You just recanted this.

Real controls are **not in the APK**: gitignore the key (same pattern as `play-upload.jks`), inject at assemble, assume anyone with the APK has the key, jail it on Headscale (ephemeral, `tag:stranger`, expiry, not reusable). That split is **M10** — M9 is not closed as a security story until M10. Limits still: no secrets in git. ControlURL (login-server URL) may be baked; it is not a secret.

- **growth (1,000):** 2 is a breach. 1/3 + Headscale tags. Funnel still Reject.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Headscale is self-host ($0 software, your always-on HTTPS). Tailscale.com keys skip IdP but are not 3’s Headscale pick (T3 already locked Headscale).
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is hours on top of tsnet AAR (the AAR is the week). Crossover: pick 3 if a stranger cannot be trusted to paste; pick 1 if the sit is an instrument they touch.
- **Crossover:** Never 2. If paste is still “a Compose window” in your mouth, pick 3.

**Dependencies:** M8=3. Unlocks **M10** (which measures). Then M3 (CURRENT must name gitignored inject + Headscale jail, not “secret safe in APK”).

### M10 — Which “security measures” for a baked AuthKey? — **decided**

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> M9=2: AuthKey lives in the APK so JOIN is a symbolic PNG with no Compose window. You asked for security measures. Fact: anyone with the APK can extract the string. CURRENT Limits already forbid secrets in git (same as the Play `.jks`). Headscale can mint **ephemeral**, **tagged**, **expiring**, **non-reusable** preauth keys; tsnet README already wants `Ephemeral=true` and `tag:stranger`. Obfuscation does not change extractability. What actually binds?

**Options presented:**

1. **Headscale-side jail + gitignored inject (recommended).** Key never in git. Assemble reads `~/.mechanicall/headscale-preauth` (or sibling of the Play keystore). APK contains the string. Treat the APK as public: key is ephemeral, `tag:stranger`, short TTL, ACL’d so a stolen node cannot Funnel, cannot see operator CURRENT, cannot WAKE unless W12 says so. Rotate by rebuilding. Same APK for closed testers until TTL. No R8-as-security.
2. **Per-sitter APK.** Factory mints a unique one-time key into each binary. 50 testers = 50 assembleDebug. Strongest identity, brutal factory.
3. **One-time baked key, then filesDir tsnet state.** First launch consumes the key; later launches use node state on device. Reinstall/sideload of the *same* APK fails after expiry. Still extractable for the first race.
4. **Obfuscation theater** (R8, NDK .so, AES asset with baked cipher key). Does not bind. Reject as the measure.
5. **Recant M9=2** back to paste CRect (1) or QR sidecar (3) after hearing the zip fact.

**Selected:** **1 — Headscale jail + gitignored inject.** User replied `M10=1`. Matches rec. Not 2–5.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Option **1**. The control plane is Headscale, not the APK. This matches how the Play upload key already lives off-git. Option 2 only if testers are named and few. Option 3 is 1 plus a race. Option 4 is how people pretend 2 is safe. Option 5 if you will not ship a string you assume is leaked.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** 1 = $0 extra; hours for inject + Headscale ACL; rebuild when TTL dies. 2 = hours × N sitters. 3 = 1 + first-run logic. 4 = weeks of fake security. 5 = undo bake.
- **alpha (50):** 1 = one APK, one tagged key, 50 ephemeral nodes if reusable-tagged; or rotate often. 2 = 50 binaries (Play internal hates this). 4 still leaked × 50.
- **growth (1,000):** 2 does not scale. 1 + Headscale tags. Funnel still Reject. Stolen Play APK = stolen key; TTL+ACL is the containment.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Headscale self-host. No DexGuard ($2000+/y) — that is still theater.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is days on top of tsnet AAR. Crossover: 1→2 when a stranger with the APK must not share a tag with other strangers *and* you will operate a factory. Never 4.
- **Crossover:** If you cannot rotate (no rebuild path), do not bake. Recant to M9=3.

**Dependencies:** M9=2. Unlocks **M11** (WAKE lamp vs wol-pi — ACL “cannot WAKE” depends on this). Then M3 (CURRENT Limits: gitignore preauth, inject at assemble, Headscale jail named).

**Status:** `decided`

### W11 — HOLD then ASLEEP (working-hours schedule)

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> After STREAM quiets, when does myarch go ASLEEP **including working hours**?

**Options presented:**

1. **N minutes after last token always**, unless a local session (you at the keyboard) is active. Working hours are not a keep-alive. **Recommended.**
2. **Always after N minutes**, even if you are at the desk (brutal).
3. **Calendar:** sleep only nights; 09–18 may stay IDLE. Fights “working hours fully asleep.”
4. **Manual only:** you suspend; protocol does not auto-sleep.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Option 1. N ≈ 10–15 min keep_alive then S3. Operator-at-desk is the only exception.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** systemd timer / `keep_alive` — hours. 3 = calendar cron, fights W7. 4 = $0 and the box stays hot.
- **SaaS:** none.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 only if you never work at myarch’s console.

**Dependencies:** W7 amendment, W10. Unlocks return path in CONJURE.md.

**Selected:** **1 — N minutes after last token unless a local session is active.** User: `W11: 1`. **N=15 minutes** recorded skip-obvious (override if you say otherwise). Working hours are not a keep-alive.

**Status:** `decided`

## Per-decision sections

### P0 — Code facts this grill will not re-ask (auto)

**Question (exactly as posed):** *(not posed — explored the tree)*

**Options presented:** none.

**Selected:** Record as fact.

**Rationale / recommendation:** Skill: if the codebase answers it, do not ask.

Facts:

1. **SOS UI failed as architecture, then the fix was reverted.** Camel Space SOS Test is a **bitmap-skinned VSTGUI editor** (`CBitmap` of the whole panel; knobs are filmstrips; hit-tests are `CRect`s). 0.15.1–0.15.2 painted millwork live in Compose. `15_binary-skin` named the VSTGUI pipeline (`drawable-nodpi/sit_skin.png` blit + overlay hits) and explicitly **did not extract Camel PNGs**. `16_revert-skin` restored cream Host Compose millwork. Live glass is the revert, not SOS.
2. **Libraries:** original authors used **VSTGUI 2.x**, not Compose `drawBehind`. JUCE `BinaryData` is Alchemy-era, not Camel Space 2005. Neither VSTGUI nor JUCE is in the APK.
3. **Drafting:** `DecideModule.kt` lists IN/OUT hunks + alt wordings. `ChatHome.kt` is still field chips + SEND. S11 said hunk-scoped ICM may auto-complete schema on PROPOSE. User now: first-time wants a **template the 7B generates**; they do **not** pick hunks from one prompt; Decide hunk picker is useless.
4. **Store:** live CURRENT **Rejects Play Store**. `STORE-GATE.md` G2 millwork FLAG, G4 mesh BLOCK. No off-the-LAN SDK test in receipts. storm-1 was killed. Lab drop is `127.0.0.1:8765` (on-LAN).
5. **Law:** `sit-one-path` proposal parks millwork and Play Store. Not applied. Dual-Next still Reject.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 to record. Re-asking “did we copy VSTGUI?” wastes the grill.
- **alpha / growth:** listing a Compose pastiche costs tester bounce, not $25.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** N/A.
- **Engineer-time:** 0.
- **Crossover:** none.

**Dependencies:** S9, S11, S0c, G2, E7, STORE-GATE. Unlocks P1.

**Status:** `decided` (auto)

### P1 — Law-shape of SOS + first-sit template + testers

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> You named three failures as one rage: SOS face never used the original toolkit, first-time Draft is not a 7B template (hunk pick landed on Decide), and you need testers via the App Store without even an off-the-LAN SDK test. Live CURRENT **Rejects Play Store**, **one Next**, and parked Camel Space as a later reskin. How should this grill become law?

**Options presented:**

1. **One new CURRENT action-id for the whole bundle (recommended).** Recant S9 (SOS face *is* this Next, VSTGUI-grammar blit, not Camel PNGs). Recant Decide-as-hunk-picker (first-sit = 7B emits a CURRENT *template*; multi-ICM; Decide stays Publish). Recant Play Reject **only as closed testing** after a named **off-the-LAN SDK test**. Human applies after this tree closes. Dual-Next still Reject.
2. **Sequential Nexts.** UI CURRENT, then draft CURRENT, then store CURRENT. One at a time after each human next. Testers wait.
3. **Play closed testing only.** Ship the current face/draft to testers this week. UI and ICM later.
4. **Sideload / off-LAN SDK only.** Keep Play Reject. Testers via APK link. SOS + template still this grill but not a listing.

**Selected:** **1 — One CURRENT for the whole bundle** (matches rec). Not sequential. Not Play-only. Not sideload-only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

YES / NOT YET stay **small** two-tap actions on that paper so B6 still holds. The Confirm **dialog goes away**. One writing widget family as Plan’s Proposal well (OutlinedTextField / paper), not a second mode.
2. **Keep pads + dialog; only restyle the Why field** to look like Notepad (font, cream paper). Input *mode* stays dialog-after-arm. Cheapest. Still theater.
3. **Recant two-tap.** Decide is Notepad.exe: the document *is* the decision; one explicit Publish after they typed a non-empty Why. First tap can be Yes if Why is already on the page. Conflicts with current B6 “first tap is not Yes” unless we rewrite BEHAVIOURS with a human CURRENT.
4. **Hand off to the OEM notepad / FILES.** Decide opens the system editor on `PROPOSE-CURRENT.md` (or a Why file). Mechanicall only has Publish after they return. Honest files. Ugly sitting.

The Why well must be on the page *before* arming, or testers never write and agents cannot demo Decide without tapping Confirm. Two-tap can remain so silence still is not permission. Standardise = same paper widget as Plan, not a new millwork family.

**Cost tradeoff:**

### S1 — Who sits, and what is a “depreciated stranger”?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> The 18 August sitting failed because Tailscale-as-me is not a send path, and the people-app lesson was: a stranger should not have to join your tailnet as you. You now want an in-app action so the **app user can accept an invitation to the tailnet**, then **boot myarch** and **send requests**, with a **depreciated stranger role**. Who is sitting in the app, and what does that role mean?

**Options presented:**

1. **Stranger-as-themselves, limited ACL (recommended).** They accept an invite under *their* identity — not `17kizymaa@`. Tailscale ACL writes them down: may reach wol-pi (wake) and myarch:11434 (Ollama), nothing else. “Depreciated stranger” = privilege written down, not operator. Invite / Wake / Send still not Yes. Matches the 2026-08-19 HANDOFF shape.
2. **Operator-only; stranger role retired.** The app user is you. Off-LAN A33 joins the tailnet so *you* can boot myarch and Send. “Depreciated stranger” means we **deprecate** the people-app fantasy of a sitter who never joins the private net. One operator device. Still not logging in as a friend.
3. **Me-as-stranger lab first.** You hold a second Tailscale identity (throwaway, not operator email) on a second device or login to prove the invite path. Same ACL as 1. Real friend later.
4. **No join.** Keep 18 August as law: strangers never join. Desk hop stays USB reverse / LAN. Invite chrome is docs. This **rejects** the spike you just named.

**Selected:** **1 — Stranger-as-themselves, limited ACL.** User picked the recommended option. Not operator-only. Not me-as-stranger lab. Not “no join.”

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.** Option 3 if no friend is in the room this week.

Why: you put “app user,” “accept an invitation,” and “depreciated stranger role” in one sentence. That is not “I join as myself.” Option 2 is the cheap reading (A33 already hops via USB reverse on LAN; Tailscale-as-you already exists) and it **erases** the word stranger. Option 4 contradicts the spike. Option 1 is the 19 August HANDOFF: Join as them, then Wake, then Send. “Depreciated” is read as **written-down ACL**, not as a typo for “deprecated” — unless you pick 2, which *is* that typo.

Code cannot answer this: `ChatHome.kt` hardcodes desk URLs `127.0.0.1` / LAN `192.168.0.51` / Tailscale `100.90.85.68`. There is **no** Join action in the APK. The 18 Aug receipt and the 19 Aug HANDOFF disagree about whether a stranger should ever be on the tailnet. Only you can pick.

Option 2 = hours if Tailscale app is already on the phone; deep-link / “open Tailscale” chrome. Option 3 = same build as 1 plus one extra identity (free). Option 4 = $0 and Send from LTE still prints `Desk quiet.`
- **alpha (50):** Operator tailnet + 50 tagged strangers is operationally a product. Tailscale Personal Plus $0–6 or Headscale ~$5 VPS. Do not put 50 people on your personal tailnet without intending to.
- **growth (1,000):** Wrong topology. At that scale the people-app lesson (no join) is cheaper than a shared Ollama tailnet. Crossover around a handful of sitters: leave 1, stop sharing the desk model.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Tailscale Personal $0 vs Headscale $0+VPS. Public Ollama $0 and an incident. No BaaS.

### S2 — How does the app user *accept* the invitation on the phone?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> S1 locked stranger-as-themselves with a limited ACL. Tailscale will not let a random APK invite people with no operator secret. The 2026-08-19 HANDOFF said: until wol-pi holds a Tailscale API token (not in git) and mints a path, a button labelled Invite is decoration. For this **quick spike**, how does Mechanicall let them accept?

**Options presented:**

1. **JOIN opens an invite URL / Tailscale app (recommended for a quick spike).** Operator (or later wol-pi) mints an invite. Mechanicall shows Join-network status: not-on-net / invited / connected. The actual accept happens in **Tailscale’s app** (or the invite link in a browser). Mechanicall then probes wol-pi + myarch. Honest. Days, not weeks. Invite is still not Yes.
2. **wol-pi mints an auth key; Mechanicall requests it.** wol-pi holds the Tailscale API token off-git. App sends *their* email; box returns an invite/auth path. User still needs Tailscale app (or option 3) to consume the key. This is the durable door. Not “quick.”
3. **Embed libtailscale in the APK.** Mechanicall *is* the Tailscale client. VPN permission, fat APK, not a quick spike. Only pick if you refuse a second app on the phone.
4. **QR of invite URL or pre-auth key.** Operator shows a QR from the desk; stranger scans in Mechanicall; that opens Tailscale (same as 1 with a sitting-room prop). Lab-friendly. Still not a secret in git.
5. **Paste key only.** They already installed Tailscale; Mechanicall is a well + “open Tailscale.” Weakest “action in the app.”

**Selected:** **1 — JOIN opens invite URL / Tailscale app.** User picked the recommended option. Not wol-pi mint this spike. Not libtailscale. Not QR. Not paste-only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1** for this spike. Keep **2** as the durable follow-on, not this week’s chrome.

Why: you said *quick spike* and *an action that allows the app user to accept*. The action can be **Join** that hands them the minted invite and then **watches the net** — that is an action, not a homework assignment at `login.tailscale.com` *inside Mechanicall’s story*. Option 2 is what HANDOFF called the honest gap; it needs a tiny always-on service + token hygiene. Option 3 is a VPN product. Option 4 is 1 with a QR. Option 5 is decoration with extra typing.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 Tailscale. 1 = 1–3 engineer days (chrome + reachability probe + ACL already on tailnet). 2 = +3–7 days wol-pi service, token in a file the APK never sees. 3 = 1–3 weeks embed + VPN UX. 4 = +0.5 day scan. 5 = hours and a lying Join button.
- **alpha (50):** 1 still means *you* mint invites (admin click or a later 2). 2 is the only way 50 invites are not your weekend. 3 is an MDM-shaped product.
- **growth (1,000):** None of these belong on the operator tailnet. Headscale or stop sharing Ollama. Dollar delta still << the support cost of 1,000 tagged strangers.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Tailscale API on wol-pi is still self-host. Do not put the token in Play-distributed APK (that is a secret in a zip).
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is the spike. 2 is the factory. 3 is a different app.

### S4 — How does the phone **boot myarch** once they are on the tailnet?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> You said accept-invite is **to enable the boot myarch and send requests**. JOIN (S2) gets them on the net. Send (S2b) talks to `100.90.85.68:11434`. myarch **sleeps**. wol-pi is already on the tailnet and can Wake-on-LAN. What is the boot action in Mechanicall for this spike?

**Options presented:**

1. **WAKE DESK via wol-pi (recommended).** Only enabled when Join=connected. Mechanicall calls a tiny tailnet-only endpoint on wol-pi that sends a magic packet to myarch’s MAC. Status: sleeping / waking / up. Probe `:11434`. Wake is not Yes. If already up, status is up and Send works.
2. **Assume myarch is already up.** JOIN only unlocks Send. Boot stays `scripts/boot-desk.sh` on the machine. Phone never WoL. Cheaper; you said “boot myarch” in the same sentence as the invite.
3. **JOIN + probe only this spike; WoL is a follow-on.** Quickest honest slice. Button labelled Wake is decoration until wol-pi HTTP exists — same lie HANDOFF warned about for Invite. Only pick if you want Join-and-Send *this week* and Wake later.
4. **SSH from the phone to myarch to start Ollama.** Requires myarch already up enough for SSH. That is not wake-from-sleep.

**Selected:** **1 — WAKE DESK via wol-pi.** User picked the recommended option. Not assume-up. Not defer WoL. Not SSH.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.** wol-pi is already the always-on box. CURRENT’s desk-hop clause is “anyone can boot this desk.” A stranger on LTE cannot run `boot-desk.sh` on myarch. Option 2 leaves them connected to a sleeping box and Send prints `Desk quiet.` Option 3 is the real “quick” if you will not stand up a 30-line HTTP on wol-pi this spike — say so. Option 4 is not boot.

Honest gap: the WoL HTTP on wol-pi is **not** in tree yet, same class as the invite mint you just deferred. Wake needs a small service + MAC + token/ACL, **not** in git. If you pick 1, this spike includes that service, not only APK chrome.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 hardware (wol-pi exists). 1 = 1–3 days wol-pi HTTP + APK button + probe. 2 = $0 extra, Send fails when the desk sleeps. 3 = 0 days WoL, a documented follow-on. 4 = hours and still no sleep-wake.
- **alpha (50):** one wol-pi still wakes one desk. 50 sitters hammering WoL is fine; 50 desks is a different product.
- **growth (1,000):** not a WoL fleet. Stop.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** a public wake URL is Funnel-shaped and **rejected**. Tailnet-only HTTP on wol-pi is $0.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is the meaning of “enable boot.” 3 is the meaning of “quick.” They fight; you pick.
- **Crossover:** 1→3 only if wol-pi SSH this week is blocked. Never a public `/wake`.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: S1=1, S2=1, S2b (Send URL).
- Unlocks: S3 (Join + Wake as two controls, not a fifth planning page), S6 (1 adds a wol-pi service — may want human re-SELECT; 3 stays under `ACTUAL-APP-VERIFICATION` more easily).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** wol-pi HTTP is in scope for this spike. Secrets (Tailscale token if any, MAC) stay off git and off the APK. Public wake URL remains Reject. GATE stays quiet until `:11434` answers.

You now have **both** JOIN (S2) and WAKE (S4). Where do they sit, and may a stranger accept the invite **before** they bind a folder?

**Options presented:**

1. **Network strip beside DIR; Join allowed unbound (recommended).** Compact DESK strip: Join status + Wake. Not a fifth rail page. Unbound still shows FOLDER as the only module, plus the strip so they can accept the invite before picking a project. Wake enabled only when connected. Gray-outside still closes *modules*, not the strip.
2. **Only after bind.** Unbound is FOLDER only — no Join. They must pick a pocket before they can accept the invite. Visually pure B1; makes the net a sitting step, not a door.
3. **FILES-style overlay** from a DESK chip. Same dimmer + gray-outside-closes rule as FILES. Strip hidden until they open DESK.
4. **Fifth rail page NETWORK.** Plan / Draft / Decide / Receipt / Net. Rejects “not a fifth planning page” unless you insist the net *is* a module.
5. **Inside Draft only.** Join/Wake appear as Send’s prerequisites. Hidden if they never open Draft. Makes the desk feel like chat infrastructure.

**Selected:** **2 — Only after bind.** User override of recommendation (was 1 — strip + Join unbound). Unbound is FOLDER only — no Join. They must pick a pocket before they can accept the invite.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.**

Why: accepting a tailnet invite is **not** binding a CURRENT. A stranger on LTE should be able to Join, then Wake, then Bind their folder, then Send. Option 2 couples two different verbs and fails the first-mile story (HANDOFF: Invite is the first mile). Option 3 hides the lamp. Option 4 becomes a verb museum. Option 5 makes Draft the network panel and fights B4 (Draft is a proposal workshop, not a modem).

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 infra. 1 = 0.5–1.5 days chrome on the existing DIR row. 2 = slightly less chrome, worse first-run. 3 = reuse FILES dialog (hours) but an extra tap. 4 = +1 day a page you already rejected as identity. 5 = pollutes ChatHome.
- **alpha / growth:** none of these are SaaS. Support cost is “where is Join,” not dollars.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** N/A.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is the smallest honest surface for two controls.
- **Crossover:** 1→3 if the strip crowds the LCD. Never 4 while four modules are the walk.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: S2=1, S4=1, B1 bind-first.
- Unlocks: S5 (Draft stays a workshop, not a modem), S6 (chrome vs new Next).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** First mile is **bind, then Join, then Wake, then Send**. HANDOFF’s “invite before the sitting” is declined. Bind-first stays the product door. JOIN/WAKE still must not become a fifth planning page — after bind they live as a compact strip beside DIR (the non-page half of rec 1). Accept/reject hunks mutate **PROPOSE only**. Decide = Publish (two-tap + Why). Desk Send inserts suggestions, not law. This is Word if the review copy is not the published file.
2. **Plan is the document in Suggesting mode.** They look at live CURRENT with suggestion marks; every edit is a suggestion whose bytes live in PROPOSE until Decide. One surface. Risk: it *looks* like they are editing law.
3. **Visual diffs only — no per-hunk accept.** Steal the look (red/green vs live). SAVE DRAFT writes the whole proposal. Fastest. Weakest “workshop” vs Docs.
4. **Accept hunk writes CURRENT.** Illegal. Same as model-Yes with extra taps. Do not pick.

Why: Docs Suggesting works because there is one doc. Mechanicall has two files on purpose. The honest steal is: **PROPOSE is the review copy**, CURRENT is published, Decide is Publish. Option 2 is more “spot-on Docs” visually and more likely to fail a STORM (“they thought the yellow text was law”). Option 3 is last-pass with prettier paint. Option 4 is prohibited.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 infra. 1 = 5–8 days hunk UI + `write_schema_draft` / PROPOSE patcher (engine already writes PROPOSE; hunks are chrome + a small Python apply-to-propose). 2 = same plus a lying Plan. 3 = 1–2 days paint. 4 = $0 and a law incident (repair >> build).
- **alpha (50):** 1 scales as “review copy vs published.” 2 scales as support tickets. 4 does not scale — it is a protocol bug.
- **growth (1,000):** still files. No Google Docs API, no Drive. Dollar delta vs real Docs: $0 vs $0–12 Drive + a second CURRENT.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** do not import Google Suggesting API. Reimplement marks on local markdown.
- **Engineer-time:** hunk accept is the week you just bought with S5b. Option 3 declines that week.
- **Crossover:** 1→3 only if hunk UI misses this calendar. Never 4. 1→2 only if they will not open two modules.

- Unlocks: S5d (what the document *is*: whole CURRENT.md vs fields vs PROPOSE fenced block), S6 (this is a redesign Next, not a verification polish).

### S10c — Device-wide VPN, or only Mechanicall’s sockets?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> S10b locked: the APK **is** the Tailscale client. Official Tailscale Android uses a **VpnService** (the whole phone joins the tailnet). tsnet/libtailscale can instead run **userspace** so only Mechanicall’s sockets (wake + Ollama) go through the tunnel. A stranger’s other apps then stay on LTE. Which embed?

**Options presented:**

1. **Userspace / app-local tunnel (recommended).** Mechanicall reaches wol-pi + myarch. Other apps do not ride your tailnet. No “this app wants to set up a VPN” system sheet — or a smaller one. Right for a document workshop and a depreciated stranger.
2. **VpnService — device-wide.** Genuine Tailscale-on-Android shape. Phone is on the tailnet. ACL `tag:stranger` is the fence for *every* app, not just Mechanicall. Heavier consent. Kid/sitter may refuse the VPN dialog.
3. **VpnService + the same ACL fence (2 with S1 taken as sufficient).** Device-wide, and you accept that Maps/Chrome also hit the tailnet unless ACL denies.
4. **Userspace now, VpnService as later settings** — same two-clock as S9’s Camel Space reskin.

**Selected:** **1 — Userspace / app-local tunnel.** User picked the recommended option. Not VpnService. Not ACL-as-only-fence-for-the-whole-phone. Not later-settings VPN.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.**

Why: S1 already wrote the stranger down to wol-pi + `:11434`. Putting *Chrome* on that tailnet is extra blast radius for no workshop gain. Userspace is enough for Send and Wake. Option 2 is the “genuine Tailscale resource” (mirrors S9 later-reskin thinking) but it is a product door a mum-class sitter may bounce on. Option 4 if you want genuine VPN later without blocking this lab.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0. 1 = still 1–3 weeks embed, easier Play/sideload story (no always-on VPN). 2 = same weeks + VPN UX + ACL must be perfect. 3 = 2. 4 = 1 now + a later settings project.
- **alpha (50):** device-wide VPN in a sideload APK is support (“why did my banking app die”). Userspace is quieter.
- **growth (1,000):** VpnService-in-app is MDM. Do not take that from lab.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** N/A. Still Tailscale Personal.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 and 2 are the same library, different Android permission surface. 1 is less QA against the rest of the phone.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 only if userspace cannot resolve MagicDNS or cannot open `:11434`. 1→4 if you want a settings toggle later.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: S10b=1, S1 ACL, S4 Wake, S2b-amended MagicDNS.
- Unlocks: S12 (sharing a pocket still ≠ sharing the tailnet); S11 (desk path is the tunnel).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Tailscale-as-component is closed for this continuation: embed + recant S2 + userspace. Device-wide VPN parked (unlike Camel Space, they did **not** ask to park it as later settings). Reopen S10c only if userspace cannot hit MagicDNS/` :11434`.

Option 4 is an operator convenience, not a sitter feature — say so if you want it as a leaf, not as “sharing.”

### S11 — How does a 7B with an active LoRA stay stable in the document workshop?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> S5d locked **whole `CURRENT.md`** as the document. Desk Send **inserts suggestions** (S5c), not law. The desk model is **`personal-llm-sft-v4`** — a **7B with an active LoRA** — already on a brutal slim ICM (`CHAT_USER_TURNS=3`, 280/240 chars, stages show-plan|propose|gate, fields only, local stage owns PROPOSE write). Dumping the whole file into that model will make it wander. How does ICM keep the workshop stable?

**Options presented:**

1. **Hunk-scoped ICM (recommended).** Never send whole CURRENT. Prompt = tiny sys + stage `propose` + **the one section/hunk in focus** + live vs proposed for that hunk + last 3 user turns. Model returns a **patch for that hunk** (not a rewritten file, not chat). LoRA remains propose/taste only. Python applies the patch to PROPOSE. GATE still streams. Matches ICM paper: load only the layer you need.
2. **Whole-file in the prompt anyway.** 7B+LoRA hallucinates Keep/Reject, “helpfully” rewrites law-shaped prose. Unstable. Don’t.
3. **Dumb section retrieve, no vectors.** Split CURRENT on headings; retrieve the named section. Still no embeddings (CORE: no vector DB in v0). Cousin of 1 with a file index.
4. **Human-only workshop this spike; desk off.** Most stable 7B. Fights “desk inserts suggestions.” Only if you will not Send in Draft.
5. **Bigger or cloud model for the workshop.** Recants local `personal-llm-sft-v4` / private desk. Conflicts with CURRENT Host.

**Selected:** **Other — option 1, and it can auto-complete the entire schema from the user’s hunks too.** User: “1 (can auto-complete the entire schema from users hunks, too).” Not whole-file prompt. Not desk-off. Not cloud. Not retrieve-only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.** Option 3 if “hunk” needs a heading map.

Why: the ICM paper and this repo’s slim chat already solved “7B gets lost in the middle.” The workshop must **not** undo that by pasting the novel. A LoRA trained to propose will over-write if you hand it the whole CURRENT. Option 2 is the fail. Option 4 is honest if the 7B cannot patch; you already wanted Send to insert suggestions. Option 5 is a different product.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0 GPU (same box). 1 = 2–5 days prompt/stage + patch parser + tests that a dumped-file path is refused. 2 = $0 and a broken workshop. 3 = +0.5 day heading index. 4 = −desk days, +weaker Draft. 5 = $20–200/mo API or a bigger GPU.
- **alpha (50):** 1 stays one 7B. 5 is the bill. 2 is 50 confused sitters.
- **growth (1,000):** still one local model per desk, not a fleet, if 1 holds. Crossover to 5 only if 7B cannot emit a hunk at all after LoRA.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** 1/3/4 = $0 Ollama. 5 = cloud and a corpus leak.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is the stability work. It is not optional if S5d stands.
- **Crossover:** 1→3 when sections are long. 1→4 if tests show the LoRA cannot emit patches. Never 2. Never 5 on this Host line.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: S5c, S5d, existing `draft_chat` slim ICM, ARCHITECTURE (LoRA = propose only), S8 research (FACE-SPEC must name the prompt contract).
- Unlocks: S13 (last); close continuation.

### T4 — GATE shows boot load + model response. How?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Today GATE is idle/warm/gate/quiet with 16 LEDs during **streamed generate**. You want timeout/boot logic whenever someone uses the local model: the **gate bar should represent boot loading time + model responses**. `personal-llm-sft-v4` may still be paging into VRAM. What is the bar?

**Options presented:**

1. **Two-phase GATE (recommended).** Phase LOAD: Ollama up + model in memory (timeout, e.g. 60s) — bar fills on load. Phase STREAM: existing step LEDs on tokens. Distinct colours or labels (LOAD vs GATE). Quiet after. Not typing dots. Not Yes. WAKE desk (magic packet) is *before* LOAD if myarch was asleep.
2. **One mixed 0–100 bar.** Load and tokens share a single fill. Harder to tell “still booting” from “still thinking.”
3. **BOOT lamp + GATE strip as two widgets.** More chrome, clearer.
4. **Keep current GATE; only add numeric timeouts.** Smallest change; does not “represent boot.”

**Selected:** **1 — Two-phase GATE (LOAD then STREAM).** User picked the recommended option. Not mixed 0–100. Not two widgets. Not timeouts-only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.** LOAD vs STREAM is what a 7B on a sleeping desk actually does. Mixing them (2) is how people think the model hung. WAKE remains the strip button; LOAD is GATE after the box is up.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0. 1 = 1–3 days engine (`write_gate` states `load`/`gate`) + strip. 2 = 1 day, worse UX. 3 = +0.5 day widgets. 4 = hours.
- **alpha:** timeouts matter when 50 sitters wake one GPU.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** still Ollama.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is T5’s prerequisite (background jobs need a LOAD queue).
- **Crossover:** 1→3 if the strip cannot show two phases. Never `…` on Send.

**Dependencies:** T1, existing GATE, WAKE S4.
**Unlocks:** T5 background calls.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** `write_gate` gains `load` then `gate`. WAKE is still the magic-packet strip. LOAD starts when the box is up. Timeouts are engine, not Yes.

### E7 — How are Headscale folder uploads/downloads handled?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Final consideration needed: how are the headscaled folder uploads/downloads handled?

**Code already true (so we do not invent EdubaWare disk):**

- Headscale is a **login-server**. It coordinates WireGuard. It does **not** store folders, CURRENT, or PROPOSE.
- Tailscale.com **Taildrop / Send** is not a Headscale product feature we can steal as law.
- Bound pocket is **local** on the phone (B10). JOIN pastes a preauth key; tsnet state is `filesDir/tsnet/`, never the Domain folder.
- Designed download: **GET-only twin** (`python/aether_twin.py` / future tsnet `Listen :7078`) of named law files into `~/mechanicall-twin/<sitter>/`. GET is not Yes. POST is 405.
- JNI tsnet is **not** in the APK yet — so even that download is not live. Lab fallback is adb pull (not STORM, not A33-as-product).
- S12: no syncthing dual-write, no git-in-pocket this sit. `aether_pocket_serve.py` Yes POST is the **brake face**, not twin.

**Options presented:**

1. **Mesh + read-twin only** (recommended). No Headscale upload. Operator **downloads** CURRENT/PROPOSE/receipts/events/gate/tailnet-fp via GET over the tailnet. Sitter **uploads** nothing to Headscale. Seed a folder with Bind (local) or a template copy — that is install, not Yes. Matches T8b + SHARING.md.
2. **Seed download once.** After JOIN, phone may **GET a template pocket** from operator (PROPOSE-shaped zip or file list) into the bind path if empty. Never overwrite live CURRENT. Still no Headscale object store.
3. **Propose-only PUT.** Twin grows POST `/propose` that writes `PROPOSE-CURRENT.md` only. Still 405 on `/current`. Easy to mistake for Yes.
4. **Bidirectional rsync/syncthing over Headscale.** Two writers. Dual-CURRENT. S12 Reject.
5. **EdubaWare-style upload folder into a container.** Recanted (E4). Don’t.

**Selected:** **Other — depreciated opt-in folder send to mechanicall-os `.inbox/` (gitignored) + operator can send folders back (new application, etc.) with user notify/Accept.** Not 1-only. Not 4 syncthing. Not 5 EdubaWare. User: “6. I want to give the users a depreciated option to upload the folder to my mechanicall-os repo (locally; .gitignored). It also allows me to send folders back full of a new application, etc. (this notifies the user). Protocols for handling this should follow standards of similar procedures of other AI platforms with this action.”

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1** as the *default* (twin GET). They added a **depreciated** extra path: local inbox, not Headscale-as-Drive. Steal AirDrop/Taildrop/ChatGPT-download: offer → notify → Accept/Decline. Accept skips live CURRENT. A new-application folder is still an offer, not auto-install Yes. Default twin GET remains.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** 1 = $0 + tsnet Listen (JNI leftover). 2 = +0.5–2 days seed GET. 3 = +1 day and a Yes-shaped footgun. 4 = split-brain repair. 5 = recant.
- **alpha (50):** 1 is one operator watching many GET endpoints. 4 is 50 dual-CURRENT incidents.
- **growth:** not a Drive.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** no BaaS. Headscale stays coordination.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 is already specified; blocked on tsnet JNI. 2 is extra. Never bake pockets into the APK.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 if first-run empty folder is too harsh (T8c grey). Never 4. Never 5.

**Dependencies:** T8b, S12, SHARING.md, E4 recant, tsnet JNI gap, B10.
**Unlocks:** amend PROPOSE `sit-storm-factory` with an explicit Headscale-bytes clause; then human re-apply.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** `python/aether_inbox.py` + `.inbox/README.md` + gitignore. JNI still must carry bytes later. FILES overlay cut → device file manager (E8).

# Archive — closed 2026-08-16 mobile-planning-demo interview

The following tree is **closed**. It is kept so S0 can inherit skip-obvious, Pocket Domain, Chaquopy, and “session is a proposal-doc editor” without re-asking. New work is the interview **above** the archive rule.

# Decision tree — mobile planning projection (phone demo spike)

**Premise:** Add an executable **spike** for a **phone projection** of Mechanicall OS: a demo you can use on your phone, live, to plan with/for a client, talking to a **remote Ollama** you serve, with a UI that honestly captures the **CLI/authority actions** — without pretending the phone is a new product core.

**Interview started:** 2026-08-16  
**Interview status:** **closed** 2026-08-16 — no remaining non-obvious forks. Spike spec: `dev/23_mobile-planning-demo/output/SPIKE.md`. Propose-current (human apply): `examples/propose-current/PROPOSE-mobile-planning-demo.md`.  
**Skill:** `grill-me-store-decisions`  
**Live law at start:** `CURRENT.md` Next = `casual-core-interface` · Phase APPROVE · Status APPROVED · Limits = design/docs this Next; heavy implement only after human approve of the contract. Non-goals on this Next include multi-surface suite expansion.

**Live law now (after D1):** `CURRENT.md` **Next field** = `mobile-planning-demo` · Phase APPROVE · Status APPROVED · two human `APPROVED` events 2026-08-16. **Body drift:** “This Next”, Action id, Brief, Limits, and Approval condition still describe `casual-core-interface`. No `next_selected` event in `.aether/events.jsonl` for the new id. Agents do **not** silently rewrite CURRENT to match.

---

## Guiding principles

Captured from the user at kickoff (elevate further if stated mid-interview):

1. **Demo build only — not a product.** Sideload APK on *your* phone for a live client sitting. Not Play Store. Not Mechanicall-core. Not a multi-user suite. D9=3 (whole-tree context) is allowed **only** while this remains a demo; if it ever stops being a demo, D9 reopens to 2.
2. **Works on my phone.** One operator device. Not a multi-user SaaS.
3. **Live client planning.** The session is real planning work, not a slide deck.
4. **Remote Ollama.** Phone consumes a model you serve (host already has B1 / `ollama-personal` / `/peer-serve`).
5. **UI must capture CLI actions.** The face has to *feel* like `aether` authority verbs, not a generic chat wrapper.
6. **Executable on a 4–8 week native calendar.** “Now” means we can *start* after this grill; first client-ready sitting is weeks, not a weekend. Still not a six-month suite or a second protocol.
7. **Law not stack** (from live CURRENT / PRODUCT). Phone is a *projection*. Filesystem remains truth. Models never approve. Silence ≠ permission.
8. **Minimize paid third parties.** Local-first; Tailscale/LAN over cloud relays; no new SaaS as authority store.
9. **This Next is `mobile-planning-demo`.** Human re-SELECTED and accepted (2026-08-16). Casual-core is paused, not cancelled. Do not treat the stale Action-id block as a second Next.
10. **Client is the planner.** They tap and say Yes. UI is mum-class (gate + plan + agent), not an operator verb museum. The phone is the workshop table.
11. **Client law lives outside this repo.** Their CURRENT is not `mechanicall-os/CURRENT.md`. The operator Next (`mobile-planning-demo`) stays on the product tree. The sitting binds a client path.
12. **Host device is the operator’s phone.** You hold the workshop. “Best setup” still means one filesystem of truth + remote Ollama — not a second copy of CURRENT.
13. **Pocket Domain.** Client CURRENT lives on the phone’s storage. `aether` runs on the phone. Ollama stays on myarch. Do not mirror a second CURRENT on the desk.
14. **Native app projection, 4–8 weeks.** The client-facing face is a real Android (assumed) app. That must remain a *projection* of the same files/`aether` law unless D12 explicitly chooses a Kotlin clone.
15. **One engine: embedded CPython + shipped `aether`.** Chaquopy (or equivalent) runs the same `python/` + `bin/aether`. Kotlin is chrome. No second parser of CURRENT.
16. **The session is a proposal-doc editor.** “Chat” is an interactive document page. The agent edits a **proposal of CURRENT** (not CURRENT). Live CURRENT + receipts + human Yes stay visible. Bubble-chat as the main face is rejected. Interaction polish is **after this sprint**.
17. **Skip obvious questions.** User 2026-08-16: “avoid obvious questions… I approve any obvious questions.” If prior law + this tree already determine the pick, record the recommendation as **decided (auto-approved)** and do not re-ask.

---

## Master cost map

Recurring **$/month**. This spike is a **single-operator demo**. Most lines are $0 if we stay on existing hardware.

| Line item | v1 (solo / this demo) | alpha (50 users) | growth (1,000 users) | Self-host alternative | Notes |
|-----------|----------------------:|-----------------:|---------------------:|-----------------------|-------|
| Ollama inference | $0 (existing desktop GPU/CPU) | $0–80 (one always-on box) | $200–2,000 (GPU fleet or API) | Keep self-host | Do **not** send client plans to cloud APIs unless chosen later |
| Phone ↔ host network | **$0 Tailscale** (D8 auto) | $0–6 Tailscale Personal Plus | $30–500 Tailscale / headscale | Headscale $0 + VPS ~$5 | Public `:11434` rejected; LAN-only fails pocket-away-from-desk |
| App store listing | $0 if PWA / sideload | $25 one-time Google Play | $25 + 15–30% if paid | Sideload / F-Droid / PWA | Demo should not wait on Play review |
| Hosted web (anphuni.com Session) | $0 unused | lab only | not core | N/A | PRODUCT: Session is **not** Mechanicall core |
| Cloud LLM (OpenRouter / xAI) | $0 if Ollama-only | $20–200 | $500–5,000 | Ollama | Conflicts with “serve my remote Ollama” unless fallback |
| Auth / BaaS (Firebase etc.) | $0 (don’t) | $0–25 | $25–200 | Files + Tailscale ACL | Would become a second authority store — reject |
| Domain / TLS if public URL | $0–12 | $12 | $12 | Tailscale Serve / MagicDNS | Prefer private |
| Engineer time (this spike) | **4–8 weeks** (native app) | — | — | — | D5=3 user override; sideload first, Play optional $25 |
| Phone backup of client Domain | $0 (syncthing / `adb backup` / git remote you already own) | $0–5 | $0–20 | Never Drive-as-authority | Only copy is on the phone until you choose a *backup*, not a second law |

**Crossover:** SaaS chat wrappers get cheaper than self-host GPU around ~200 always-on users. Irrelevant to a one-phone demo. The cost that actually matters now is **your week**, not dollars.

## Per-decision sections

### D1 — Where does this spike live so we can execute it without lying about CURRENT?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> CURRENT Next is `casual-core-interface` (APPROVED, design/docs first). Casual-core non-goals on this Next include multi-surface suite expansion. You asked to add a **spike we can execute now**. Where does that spike live, legally?

**Options presented:**

1. **Lab spike only.** Park the work under `dev/NN_mobile-planning-projection/` (plus this `decision-tree.md`). CURRENT Next stays `casual-core-interface`. We do **not** rewrite law. After the grill, a human may approve a *propose-current* appendix or just run the spike as lab. Fastest honest “execute now.”
2. **Casual-core projection clause.** The missing `docs/CASUAL-CORE-INTERFACE.md` (claimed accepted in CURRENT, file not in tree after 2026-08-11 tree-org) gains an explicit “phone projection” slice: same core (agent + Domain + Yes/Not yet), one extra face. Spike implements that slice only after you approve the contract. Slower; keeps product story coherent.
3. **Human re-SELECT Next** to something like `mobile-planning-demo`. This becomes the only Next. I will not run `aether next` / `approve`. You would. Cleanest for “this is what we are doing,” heaviest process, and it *pauses* casual-core.
4. **Client pitch document only.** No phone app. A written proposal you walk through. Zero build. Does **not** capture CLI actions on a phone.

**Selected:** **3 — Human re-SELECT Next to `mobile-planning-demo`.** User: “re-selected. accepted.” Live `CURRENT.md` **Next:** field is now `mobile-planning-demo`. Two human APPROVED events logged 2026-08-16T08:23Z. **User override** of recommendation (was 1 + appendix of 2).

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1**, with a one-page appendix shaped like option 2.

Why: you said *demo*, *my phone*, *execute now*. Live CURRENT forbids treating a new surface as this Next’s product work. We already have a projection pattern (`aether panel` → `.aether/PANEL.md` + `panel.html`) and a working B1 Ollama path. A lab spike can reuse those without claiming “Mechanicall is now a mobile app.” Option 2 is the right *product* home later; option 3 is correct only if you want to stop casual-core this week; option 4 fails your “UI must capture CLI actions” principle.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0. Option 1 = ~hours–days of your time after the grill. Option 2 = extra 1–2 days writing the contract before any phone pixels. Option 3 = process time + you pause the approved Next. Option 4 = $0 and no spike.
- **alpha (50):** If we accidentally pick 3 and ship “the Android app is the product,” we inherit store, support, and dual-authority costs ($0–50/mo + weeks). Option 1 keeps that door closed.
- **growth (1,000):** Native app + hosted backend would be the first real dollar line ($500+/mo or a GPU fleet). Do not unlock that from a demo decision.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Irrelevant at v1 if Ollama stays on your desk. Delta vs OpenRouter: $0 vs ~$20–200/mo the moment the live client session leaks to a cloud model.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 = 0.5–2 weeks build after decisions. 2 = +3–5 days contract. 3 = +1 day process, same build. 4 = 0 build weeks.
- **Crossover:** Switch from 1→2 when the casual-core contract is the thing you want the client to see *as* the product. Switch 1→3 only if casual-core is blocked and the phone demo is the only work that matters this week.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: live CURRENT (Next, Limits, Reject).
- Unlocks: D2 audience/demo moment; D3 which Domain the phone binds; D4 verb subset vs full CLI; D5 delivery vehicle (PWA vs native); D6 Ollama path; D7 write vs propose authority on device.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Recommendation was 1. Human overrode to 3 and set `**Next:** mobile-planning-demo`. CURRENT body was **not** rewritten (Action id still `casual-core-interface`). Events have approve but no `next_selected` row. That is recorded, not auto-fixed. Casual-core remains Keep/Done inventory, not this Next.

---

### D2 — Who sits in the live session, and what must they walk away believing?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Next is now `mobile-planning-demo`. Who is in the room (or on the phone), and after ten minutes what must they believe that a chat screenshot cannot prove?

**Options presented:**

1. **You + the client as planner.** They tap / speak / say Yes. They must believe *they* can stay in control of an agent while a real plan writes itself. Phone is the workshop table.
2. **You + a peer watching; you plan for a client.** The peer must believe Mechanicall is a planning *protocol* (Next, refuse, human Yes), not a chatbot. The client Domain is the content. You drive.
3. **You solo, rehearsal.** No second human. The phone must be good enough that a later client session will not embarrass you. Spike is a dress rehearsal.
4. **You + outsider glance** (friend, investor, “look at this”). They must believe a product exists. Theater. Lowest fidelity allowed; weakest proof of planning utility.

**Selected:** **1 — You + the client as planner.** User: `1`. Matches the recommendation’s “if they participate” branch (default rec was 2).

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 2**, unless the “someone else” *is* the client — then **1**.

Your kickoff was “show someone else the planning utility” *and* “plan things with it, live for the client.” That parses as: audience ≠ work-object. A peer seeing a real client Domain get a Next, a preflight refuse, and a human Yes is the unique demo. Option 1 is correct only if they participate. Option 3 wastes the “show someone” clause. Option 4 fails “capture the CLI actions” — a pretty chat is enough for theater.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0. 2 needs one extra human and a clean client Domain folder (hours). 1 needs larger Yes/Not-yet targets and fewer operator verbs (1–2 extra UI days). 3 is cheapest build, no social proof. 4 can be a static panel.html.
- **alpha (50):** Shipping 1-as-product means mum-class gate UX (casual-core again) — weeks. Shipping 2-as-product stays operator-grade. Shipping 4-as-product is a landing page, not Mechanicall.
- **growth (1,000):** 1 implies support, accounts, and a hosted face ($500+/mo or a fleet). 2 can stay self-host + Tailscale forever.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Audience 4 tempts anphuni.com Session (already rejected as core). 1/2 stay on your Ollama. Dollar delta at v1: $0 vs opening a hosted-seat line.
- **Engineer-time:** 2 ≈ baseline spike. 1 ≈ +2–4 days touch/readability. 3 ≈ −1 day polish. 4 ≈ −3 days (static).
- **Crossover:** Switch 2→1 the moment the someone else must *use* the gate, not watch it. Switch any→4 only if the meeting is a glance, not a plan.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D1 (this is now the Next, so the demo *is* the work).
- Unlocks: D3 which Domain; D4 verb subset (1 needs fewer verbs); D7 authority on device; D10 look; D9 privacy (1 exposes more to the client’s eyes).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Client participates. Spike UI must be large-gate, short copy, no operator chrome. Unlocks D3 as a *safety* question: their Yes must not apply to root `mobile-planning-demo`.

---

### D3 — Whose CURRENT is on the phone when they say Yes?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> The client is the planner. If the phone is bound to this repo’s CURRENT, they will see Next `mobile-planning-demo` and can approve *our* work. Whose folder is law on the device?

**Options presented:**

1. **Named client Domain in this tree** (e.g. `domains/<client>/` or a new folder we create). Phone binds there. Their CURRENT is their plan. Root CURRENT stays the operator law for building the spike. `domains/` is already labelled LAB.
2. **Root `mechanicall-os` CURRENT.** They plan *this product*. Rejected for D2=1 unless the client is actually helping design Mechanicall.
3. **Disposable workshop Domain.** A fresh folder (`examples/workshop-YYYYMMDD/` or similar) with a real CURRENT, sanitized or invented project. First sitting is protocol-real, corpus-safe. Can graduate to 1 or 4 later.
4. **Client project outside this repo.** A path on your disk that is *their* work (existing job folder). Phone binds there. Cleanest “this is your plan” if those files already exist.

**Selected:** **4 — Client project outside this repo.** User: `4 - on the host phone (mine; best setup)...` Override of recommendation (was 3 first sitting / 4 if files already exist). Amendment: the sitting is hosted on the operator’s phone; they want the best topology for that (asked next as D6, not assumed).

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 3** for the first live sitting, **4** if you already have their project on disk, **1** if you are starting their Mechanicall home today.

Why: D2=1 makes option 2 a law bug — their Yes would actualise `mobile-planning-demo` on the product tree. Root Keep (“no child Domain as session authority”) means *this operator session* does not grow a sub-CURRENT theatre; it does **not** mean the phone cannot bind `aether current /path/to/client`. That path form already exists. Option 3 is the cheapest way to prove the loop without leaking a real client corpus to a demo Ollama session. Switch to 4 when the meeting is billable work, not a first showing.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0. 3 = ~30–60 minutes to seed a workshop CURRENT. 1 = same plus a name and some real scope. 4 = $0 if the folder exists; +hours if you must copy/sanitize. 2 = $0 and a mistaken approve that pollutes product law (expensive in *repair time*, not dollars).
- **alpha (50):** 1-as-default implies many Domain folders on one host (still $0) plus backup/privacy process. 4-as-default implies you hold client disks (insurance/trust cost, not SaaS). 2-as-default does not scale — one CURRENT cannot be 50 clients.
- **growth (1,000):** Multi-tenant CURRENT on one phone is a hosted product ($500+/mo or a fleet). Do not unlock from this spike. Stay one-folder-per-sitting.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** 4 tempts “put their repo in the cloud so the phone can see it.” Delta vs local bind: $0 vs GitHub/Drive as a second authority store. Reject the cloud copy unless they already live there.
- **Engineer-time:** Binding is a path argument (`aether … <path>`) — hours, not weeks. The cost is *content and privacy*, not UI.
- **Crossover:** 3→4 when the sitting is real work. 3→1 when they want a lasting Mechanicall home. Never 2 while D2=1.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D1 (operator Next is the spike), D2 (client taps Yes).
- Unlocks: D9 privacy (3 is safest); D4 verbs (same loop either way); D6/D8 (where that folder is served from); D7 (whose files the phone may write).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Do not bind the phone to `mechanicall-os`. Client Yes writes their tree. “On the host phone” is **not** yet “CURRENT bytes live in `/sdcard`.” That is D6. Recommendation 3 was declined in favour of real client work — privacy (D9) gets heavier.

---

### D6 — Best setup on your phone: where do files, aether, and Ollama sit?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> You chose a client project outside this repo, hosted on *your* phone, and asked for the best setup. Remote Ollama still stands. Where do the client’s CURRENT bytes live, where does `aether` run, and where does the model run?

**Options presented:**

1. **Thin phone (recommended default).** Client folder lives on **myarch** (or another disk you already use for that job) — *outside* `mechanicall-os`. `aether` runs on myarch against that path. Ollama runs on myarch. Phone is a mum-class projection (PWA/panel face) + Yes/Not yet. One tree. Matches B1. “Host phone” = you hold the UI they touch.
2. **Pocket Domain.** Client CURRENT + files live **on the phone’s storage**. `aether` must run on the phone (Termux or an embedded port). Ollama stays on myarch (remote). Best if you leave the desk. Costs: aether-on-Android, backup, and a fat finger next to the only copy of their plan.
3. **Phone-only.** Files + aether + a small on-device model. Breaks “remote Ollama” unless you revoke that principle. Weakest planning model; simplest offline.
4. **SSH viewport.** Phone is Termux/JuiceSSH into myarch. Files never leave the desk. Looks like a CLI. Conflicts with D2=1 (client as planner, mum-class). Fine as *your* escape hatch, not their face.
5. **Mirrored copy.** Phone holds a working copy; desktop is “real.” Two CURRENTs. Reject unless you enjoy merge fights mid-meeting.

**Selected:** **2 — Pocket Domain.** User: `2!` Override of recommendation (was 1 / thin phone). CURRENT + files on phone storage; `aether` on-device; Ollama remote on myarch. Not a desktop mirror.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1.** That is the “best setup” for D2=1 + D3=4 + remote Ollama + existing B1.

Why: Mechanicall’s product is one folder of truth. myarch already serves Ollama and runs `aether`. The phone already works as a second device on that path. Putting CURRENT on the phone (option 2) is the right *pocket* setup later, but it is a second port of the protocol and a week of Termux/Android, not a spike you execute after this grill. Option 3 drops your stated model. Option 4 fails the client-as-planner face. Option 5 is a dual-authority bug.

If the meeting is *away from the desk* and the phone is the only machine in the room, say so — then the honest pick is **2**, not 1 with a prayer.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0 infra for 1 (reuse B1 + panel projection). 2 = 3–10 extra engineer-days (Termux aether or embed) + phone backup habit. 3 = $0 money, worse model, +on-device RAM. 4 = $0, no new UI. 5 = $0 and a mid-session split-brain (repair time >> build time).
- **alpha (50):** 1 stays one host + many folders ($0–80 electricity). 2 means 50 phones are sources of truth (support nightmare, still $0 SaaS). 3 does not scale quality. 5 becomes a sync product ($0 or a Dropbox line — reject as authority store).
- **growth (1,000):** 1 + Tailscale is still self-host. 2-as-product is a mobile OS ($25 Play + weeks + support). Crossover: only move files onto the phone when the sitting *cannot* see myarch.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** 1/2/4 stay $0. Temptation on 2 is “just put the folder in Drive.” That is a second CURRENT. Delta: $0 vs $0–12 Drive + a lying receipt.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 = days (projection UI). 2 = +1 week minimum. 3 = +days model ops. 4 = hours (no mum UI). 5 = never done, only regretted.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 when you regularly plan off-LAN and cannot Tailscale to the desk. Never 1→5.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D1 (spike is the Next), D2 (client taps), D3 (external client path), principle 4 (remote Ollama), principle 12 (you hold the phone).
- Unlocks: D5 delivery vehicle (1 → PWA/projection; 2 → Termux or native; 4 → no new app); D7 write path; D8 network; D9 where the corpus sits; D4 verbs (same five either way).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** User overrode “best setup = thin phone.” The sitting can leave the desk. Spike now includes an on-phone `aether` (or an honest subset that still writes the same files). D5 is blocking. D9: the only copy of their plan is in your pocket until a *backup* exists — backup ≠ second CURRENT.

### D5 — How does aether + the mum face run on the phone?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Pocket Domain means `aether` must touch files on the phone. The client must see a mum-class face, not a raw shell. How do we run that without a second implementation of law?

**Options presented:**

1. **Termux + real `aether` + local panel/HTML.** Install POSIX `aether` in Termux, bind the client folder on phone storage, `aether panel` or a tiny localhost page. Fastest honest spike. Face is “dev” unless we theme it hard.
2. **Termux `aether` + Android WebView wrapper.** Same law as 1 (one shell `aether`). Wrapper is the mum face (big Yes/Not yet, plan, chat). Termux stays the engine. Extra 1–3 days. Recommended for D2=1.
3. **Native port.** Reimplement preflight/approve/CURRENT in Kotlin (or embed Python via Chaquopy). Second law. 4–8 weeks. Reject for a spike.
4. **Chrome PWA only.** No on-device `aether`. Browser cannot faithfully write the Domain folder. Fails Pocket Domain + “capture CLI actions.”
5. **Termux raw CLI only.** `aether shell` in a terminal. $0 extra UI. Client-as-planner will not tap this.

**Selected:** **3 — Native app, 4–8 weeks.** User: `App projection is... 3! Let's do this for 4-8 weeks...` Override of recommendation (was 2; 3 was labelled “reject for a spike”). They named it **app projection** — vehicle is native; whether the *engine* is still `aether` is D12, not assumed to be a Kotlin clone.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 2.** One `bin/aether`. One folder. A face the client can tap.

Why: D6=2 forbids “phone is only a browser.” D2=1 forbids “phone is only a terminal.” Option 1 is acceptable if “execute this weekend” beats polish — we can wrap later. Option 3 is the habitat overfit casual-core just escaped. Option 4 cannot be the only copy of CURRENT. Option 5 is your escape hatch, not their workshop.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0. 1 = 1–3 days if Termux + `aether` already almost runs. 2 = +1–3 days WebView/TWA. 3 = 4–8 weeks + a second bug surface. 4 = days of UI that cannot keep law. 5 = hours.
- **alpha (50):** 2-as-product still means “install Termux + wrapper” — not shippable to 50 clients. 3 is what 50 users would need ($25 Play + support). Stay honest: this spike is *your* phone.
- **growth (1,000):** Only 3 looks like an app store product. Do not take that crossover from a demo sitting.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** All five $0 if Ollama stays on myarch. Wrapper must not route chat through a cloud BaaS.
- **Engineer-time:** 2 is the minimum mum-class pocket. 1 is the minimum *lawful* pocket. 3 is a rewrite.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 before the first client sitting if they will hold the phone. 2→3 only if you later productize Android and still generate CURRENT via the same file schema (not a new protocol).

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D6=2 (aether on phone), D2=1 (mum face), D3=4 (folder outside this repo, on device).
- Unlocks: D4 verb buttons on that face; D7 which of those buttons write; D8 how Termux reaches myarch:11434; D10 layout.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Calendar is 4–8 weeks. Principle 6 amended. D2=1 + D6=2 + D5=3 = mum-class native face over a pocket Domain. The dangerous reading of option 3 (“reimplement preflight in Kotlin”) is **not** locked. User said *projection*. D12 must close that.
