### D12 — Native for 4–8 weeks: projection of aether, or a second law?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> You chose a native app projection and a 4–8 week build. Option 3’s label also said “reimplement preflight/approve/CURRENT in Kotlin.” Does the app *call* the same `aether`, or does Kotlin become a second protocol?

**Options presented:**

1. **UI projection + exec `bin/aether`.** Compose/Flutter (or similar) is chrome only. Approve / preflight / next / current are subprocess (or bundled POSIX) of the same scripts as desktop. One law. Packaging is the hard 4–8 weeks.
2. **Embedded CPython (Chaquopy / similar).** APK ships the same `python/` + `bin/aether`. One law. Heavier APK; fewer “shell on Android” fights.
3. **Kotlin (or Dart) protocol clone.** Re-parse CURRENT, re-code preflight/approve/events/next. Same *filenames*, second *implementation*. Full 4–8 weeks of dual-bug risk. Only pick this if you want the app to survive without shipping POSIX aether.
4. **On-device daemon.** Termux or a tiny service runs real `aether`; the app is HTTP/localhost chrome. Same as 1 with a clearer process split. Extra moving part.

**Selected:** **2 — Embedded CPython (Chaquopy / similar).** User: `2`. Matches recommendation. APK ships the same `python/` + `bin/aether`. Kotlin/Compose (or similar) is chrome only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 2**, with **1** as fallback if Chaquopy is ugly on your device.

Why: you said *app projection*. In this repo that word already means “face of the files,” not “new authority.” PRODUCT and casual-core both refuse a second stack. A Kotlin clone will drift from `bin/aether` the first time SPEC-v0.2 gains a field — and the client Yes would depend on which parser they hit. 4–8 weeks is enough time to make a *face* honest; it is not enough time to make two laws stay twins. Option 4 is fine if you already live in Termux. Option 3 is the one I still recommend you refuse.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0 vendors. 1/2/4 = 4–8 weeks of UI + bind + Ollama client + packaging. 3 = same calendar *plus* a permanent dual-maintenance tax (every `aether` change twice).
- **alpha (50):** 1/2 still sideload-your-phone. 3-as-product looks “more Android-native” and becomes the only copy of law 50 people trust — then a desktop `aether` disagree is a support incident ($0 SaaS, high repair).
- **growth (1,000):** Clone (3) is what an app-store product tempts. Crossover only if desktop CLI is abandoned. You have not abandoned it.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Irrelevant if the engine is files. Clone tempts Room/SQLite “for speed” — that is a second store. Delta: $0 vs a lying receipt.
- **Engineer-time:** 2 ≈ 4–8 weeks (your stated budget) with one protocol. 3 ≈ 4–8 weeks now + 20–40% forever. 1 ≈ same as 2 if process-exec works; more if Android kills subprocesses.
- **Crossover:** Stay 2/1 until `aether` cannot run on-device at all. Then 4. Never 3 while desktop `aether` is still the operator tool.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D5=3 (native calendar), D6=2 (files on phone), principle 7 (law not stack), user’s word “projection.”
- Unlocks: D4 verb surface (buttons call aether vs call Kotlin); D7 write path; D8 network to myarch Ollama; D10 look; language/toolkit (Compose vs Flutter) as a *later* leaf, not law.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Law stays `bin/aether`. A contract test (app approve vs CLI approve → same events.jsonl shape) belongs in the 4–8 week plan. Toolkit (Compose vs Flutter) is still a leaf.

### D4 — Which CLI actions does the client actually see?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> The app calls real `aether`. The client is the planner. `AETHER_VERBS` is a long list. Which verbs are on the face, and which stay operator-only?

**Options presented:**

1. **Authority five + chat only.** Always-on CURRENT (objective / Next / phase). Buttons: preflight, approve, reject, next. Chat to remote Ollama (peer/propose). No seed/garden/distill/panel/shell. Smallest honest Mechanicall.
2. **Five + visible receipts.** Same as 1, plus read-only `brief` / last events / `drift`. They can *see* the trail that proves it is not a chatbot. No extra writers.
3. **Full `AETHER_VERBS` keypad.** Every CLI action as a button. Captures the CLI literally. Overwhelms a client. Extra week. Reject for D2=1.
4. **Panel split.** Main face = 2. **Advanced** (hold / operator PIN) = distill, seed, event, artifact, garden, … You can operate mid-sitting without handing them the museum.

**Selected:** **2 — Five + visible receipts**, with a user amendment that replaces “chat as chatbot.” User (prior turn): `2...` + chat like a session, agent edits a proposal of CURRENT on a page, refine after sprint. User (this turn): the session is **mainly an editor** — an interactive doc editor session/page where the agent edits the proposal document.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 2.**

Why: PRODUCT’s five questions *are* the planning utility you want to show. Receipts (`events`, brief, drift) are how they believe a Yes happened. Option 1 is almost enough but a chat-only screen still looks like a wrapper. Option 3 fails mum-class. Option 4 is the right *operator* escape if you already know you will need `seed` mid-meeting — pick 4 if you are also the operator in the same sitting (you are). Slight lean: **2 now, 4 if you refuse to leave the app for your own tools.**

Explored instead of asking a longer list: `bin/aether` verbs and `aether_panel.py` already split “main board = human gates” vs “Advanced… full power list.” Option 4 is that pattern on a phone.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1 (solo):** $0. 1 = baseline UI weeks. 2 = +0.5–1 day of read-only surfaces (panel already projects events). 3 = +1 week of buttons nobody should tap. 4 = +1–2 days for a hidden Advanced pane.
- **alpha (50):** 3-as-product is an admin console. 1/2 stay casual-core-shaped. 4 is how you support them without giving them `deinit`.
- **growth (1,000):** Same split. Do not ship 3.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Irrelevant. Extra verbs do not add vendors.
- **Engineer-time:** 2 fits inside 4–8 weeks. 3 eats the UX budget. 4 is cheap if we steal panel’s Advanced list rather than invent one.
- **Crossover:** 2→4 the first time you need `seed` or `event` while they hold the phone. Never 2→3.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D2=1 (mum), D12=2 (buttons *are* aether), existing verb list and panel split.
- Unlocks: D7 (which of those buttons may write); D10 look (five + receipts vs keypad); D11 whether Advanced later becomes casual-core.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Main face is not a message bubble list. It is a live proposal document (same role as `examples/propose-current/PROPOSE-TEMPLATE.md`). Agent may write/edit that file only. Authority five + receipts remain on the face so Yes still looks like Mechanicall, not Google Docs. D10 closed by this amendment. D7 must still lock what Yes does to CURRENT.

### D10 — Look / session shape (closed with D4)

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> Panel dual-pane vs chat-first vs verb keypad — superseded by the user’s editor-session amendment.

**Options presented (original open list):**

1. Panel dual-pane (plan + chat + human gate)
2. Chat-first bubbles
3. Verb keypad
4. **(User amendment) Interactive proposal-doc editor session/page** — agent edits PROPOSE; CURRENT/receipts/gates remain visible; refine after sprint

**Selected:** **4 — proposal-doc editor session.** Not 2 (bubbles). Not 3 (keypad). Not a Grok-left/CURRENT-right habitat clone.

**Rationale / recommendation:** This is what they asked for. Repo already has the artifact: propose-current is a file, not a chat transcript. The 4–8 week sprint ships a working editor + gates; pixel choreography later.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0. Editor-page is the UX budget of the 4–8 weeks (not an add-on). Bubble UI would waste that budget on the wrong metaphor.
- **alpha / growth:** A doc-shaped plan is easier to export/`cat` than a chat transcript. Chat-as-product would fight PRODUCT (“chat is ephemeral unless it lands as CURRENT + events”).
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Do not put the editor on Notion/Google Docs. That is a second authority store. Delta: $0 vs $0–12 and a lying receipt.
- **Engineer-time:** Editor + aether apply path is the sprint. Refine-after means we do not spend weeks on OT/CRDT.
- **Crossover:** Add richer chat *around* the doc only after the first sitting if they miss a transcript.

**Dependencies:** Depends on D4=2. Unlocks D7 (Yes vs apply vs editor-save).

**Status:** `decided`

### D7 — What does Yes do, if the session is an editor?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> The agent edits a proposal document. The client is the planner. Saving the editor must not become law. When they tap Yes, what writes, and what stays a draft?

**Options presented:**

1. **Yes = apply the visible proposal into pocket CURRENT + `aether approve`.** One human tap actualises the draft and records APPROVED. Agent never writes CURRENT. `next` stays a separate control if they re-SELECT. Cleanest mum-class. **Recommended for this sprint.**
2. **Two taps.** Yes / Accept only marks the proposal accepted (or writes a receipt). A second **Apply** (or `aether next`) changes CURRENT. More protocol-faithful. Easier to refuse mid-apply. Heavier for a client.
3. **Editor save is law.** Autosave/agent keystrokes write CURRENT. Reject. That is model-as-authority.
4. **Sprint rail only.** Lock: agent writes PROPOSE only; human Yes is the only CURRENT writer; exact apply-vs-approve-vs-next split refined after sprint. App still needs *a* Yes this sprint — treat as 1 unless you pick 2.

**Selected:** **1 — Yes applies the visible proposal into pocket CURRENT + `aether approve`.** User: `1...` plus “avoid obvious questions… I approve any obvious questions.”

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 1**, with option 4’s rail (polish later).

Why: D2=1 requires their Yes to *do something they can see* on the live CURRENT page. Option 3 is prohibited by PRODUCT. Option 2 is correct Mechanicall on a desktop; on a phone with a client it feels like a trick. The propose-current template already lists Apply / Reject / Revise — Yes maps to Apply+approve; Not yet maps to Reject. `aether next` can wait on Advanced or a second sitting.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0. 1 = one apply path (days, inside 4–8 weeks). 2 = two-step UX (+1–2 days + more explanation). 3 = $0 and a law bug. 4 without a default = an app with a dead button.
- **alpha (50):** 1 is casual-core-shaped. 2 is operator-shaped. 3 is a recall incident (plan changed while they blinked).
- **growth:** Applying a proposal is still files — no SaaS. A cloud “suggest edits” API would be the dollar line ($20–200/mo). Stay on your Ollama.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Editor must write a local PROPOSE file, then Yes shells into `aether`. Not a Firebase draft.
- **Engineer-time:** 1 fits the sprint. 2 spends polish they asked to defer. 3 looks faster and is forbidden.
- **Crossover:** 1→2 if the first sitting shows they want to accept a draft without changing Next yet.

**Dependencies:**

- Depends on: D2=1, D4=2, D10 editor, D12=2 (`aether approve` / apply via Chaquopy), `examples/propose-current/PROPOSE-TEMPLATE.md`.
- Unlocks: D8 (Ollama must reach the editor); D9 (proposal + CURRENT are the corpus on the phone); implementable spike spec.

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** `next` is not on the main Yes. Not yet = reject / leave CURRENT. Editor save stays PROPOSE.

### D9 — What may the remote Ollama read?

**Question (exactly as posed):**

> The pocket Domain may hold a real client corpus. Ollama on myarch will see whatever we send. What is in the model context each editor turn?

**Options presented:**

1. **CURRENT + PROPOSE + a short events tail only.** Smallest honest planning context. Safest. May miss project facts.
2. **Those + files the human attaches or @mentions** in the editor session. Model stays hungry unless they feed it. **Recommended.**
3. **Whole pocket tree every turn.** Simplest build. Fattest. Easiest to leak a contract they did not mean to show the model (the model is on *your* desk, but the sitting is theirs).

**Selected:** **3 — Whole pocket tree every turn.** User: `3. App projection is only a demo build!` Override of recommendation (was 2). Bound to principle 1: demo APK only.

**Rationale / recommendation:**

**Recommend option 2.** This is the one fork that is *not* obvious: quality of the live plan vs what leaves the phone toward myarch. 1 is safer and may stall the sitting. 3 is how you accidentally ingest their entire folder into GPU memory and logs. 2 matches “human Yes / human attach” — they choose what the model may know.

**Cost tradeoff:**

- **v1:** $0. 1 = cheapest tokens/RAM. 2 = +attach UX (1–2 days). 3 = less UX, more RAM, worse leak.
- **alpha:** 3-as-default is how 50 clients’ PDFs end up in one host’s Ollama logs. 2 scales as a habit.
- **growth:** Full-tree ingest is a GPU bill ($200–2,000) and a retention policy. 2 stays cheap.
- **SaaS vs self-host:** Still your Ollama. The cost is *trust*, not a vendor. Cloud LLM would add $20–200/mo and is already rejected.
- **Engineer-time:** 2 is inside the sprint. 3 looks faster and is the wrong default.
- **Crossover:** 2→1 if the first client is secretive. 2→3 never as default; optional “send this folder” is just 2 with a bulk attach.

**Dependencies:** D6=2, D8=Tailscale, D7=1, principle 4. Unlocks spike spec (context packer).

**Status:** `decided`

**Post-select note:** Full-tree ingest is a **demo shortcut**, not a product default. Ollama stays on myarch (not cloud). If the sitting’s pocket folder holds more than they intend the model to see, shrink the folder — do not add an attach UI this sprint. Crossover: reopen D9→2 the moment this is more than a demo build.
